{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-5-5","id":"msg_011CgDaQpDG2Pm7cU2VuGxqQ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Command-Transaction.md ===\n# FEATURE: Command Transaction\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Runs every ledger command inside exactly one database transaction that commits on success and rolls back on failure. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Database-Location.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides    | run_command(db, command) |\n| Consumes    | open_database, db.accounts.create, db.accounts.list |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery command that reads or writes the store runs inside one transaction. A command either completes in full or changes nothing.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `run_command(db, command)` opens one transaction on `db`, calls `command(db)` once, commits when the call returns, and returns the value the command returned.\n- If `command` raises, every write made through `db` during that call is rolled back, and the exception propagates unchanged.\n- A persistence error raised while the transaction is open, including a write lock held by another connection, rolls back the same way and propagates. The CLI maps it to exit 1 through FEATURE-Operational-Errors.md.\n- The wrapper does not commit or roll back inside a command body. Commands never open their own transaction.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC command-transaction-commits ===\nIntent: A command that returns normally has its write persisted and its return value passed back to the caller.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom transaction import run_command\n\naccount_code = \"TXNA1\"\naccount_name = \"Commit Test\"\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    result = run_command(db, lambda d: d.accounts.create(account=account_code, name=account_name))\n    assert result.account == account_code\n    matches = [row for row in db.accounts.list() if row.account == account_code]\n    assert len(matches) == 1\n    assert matches[0].name == account_name\n=== END AC command-transaction-commits ===\n\n=== AC command-transaction-rolls-back-all-writes ===\nIntent: When a command raises after two writes, neither write persists and the exception reaches the caller.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom transaction import run_command\n\nfirst_code = \"TXNB1\"\nsecond_code = \"TXNB2\"\n\nclass Abort(Exception):\n    pass\n\ndef write_then_fail(d):\n    d.accounts.create(account=first_code, name=\"Rollback First\")\n    d.accounts.create(account=second_code, name=\"Rollback Second\")\n    raise Abort(\"forced after two writes\")\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    before = len(db.accounts.list())\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        run_command(db, write_then_fail)\n    except Abort:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n    after = db.accounts.list()\n    assert len(after) == before\n    assert all(row.account not in (first_code, second_code) for row in after)\n=== END AC command-transaction-rolls-back-all-writes ===\n\n=== AC command-transaction-propagates-exception ===\nIntent: The exception raised by a failing command is the same object the caller receives, not a wrapped or swallowed error.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom transaction import run_command\n\nraised_error = RuntimeError(\"forced by criterion\")\n\ndef fail(d):\n    raise raised_error\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    try:\n        run_command(db, fail)\n    except RuntimeError as caught:\n        assert caught is raised_error\n    else:\n        raise AssertionError(\"run_command swallowed the command's exception\")\n=== END AC command-transaction-propagates-exception ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- No module begins or commits a transaction outside `run_command`.\n- A failed command never leaves a partial write in any table.\n- No command body commits its own work.\n- Application code reaches the store only through the `persistence` package, never through a database driver.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Schema-Init.md ===\n# FEATURE: Schema Init\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Implements `ledger init`, which drops and recreates the three ledger tables empty. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Command-Transaction.md, FEATURE-CLI-Dispatch.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides    | ledger init |\n| Consumes    | open_database, run_command |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`ledger init` resets the store to three empty tables, so a later load or report starts from a known state.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `ledger init` drops `accounts`, `categories`, and `transactions` if they exist, then recreates them empty from the generated schema through the `persistence` package.\n- The drop and recreate run inside one command transaction (FEATURE-Command-Transaction.md).\n- `init` writes nothing to standard output and exits 0.\n- Argument-count errors are rejected by FEATURE-CLI-Dispatch.md before this handler runs.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC schema-init-exit-and-silent ===\nIntent: `ledger init` exits 0 and writes nothing to standard output on a fresh database.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ndsn = os.path.join(tempfile.mkdtemp(), \"ledger.db\")\nos.environ[\"DATABASE_URL\"] = dsn\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"init\")\nassert code == 0\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC schema-init-exit-and-silent ===\n\n=== AC schema-init-drops-existing-rows ===\nIntent: Running `ledger init` against a store holding a row leaves all three tables empty.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\nfrom persistence import open_database\n\ndsn = os.path.join(tempfile.mkdtemp(), \"ledger.db\")\nos.environ[\"DATABASE_URL\"] = dsn\n\nseeded = open_database(dsn)\nseeded.accounts.create(account=\"INITA\", name=\"Before Init\")\nassert len(seeded.accounts.list()) == 1\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"init\")\nassert code == 0\nassert out == \"\"\n\nafter = open_database(dsn)\nassert len(after.accounts.list()) == 0\nassert len(after.categories.list()) == 0\nassert len(after.transactions.list()) == 0\n=== END AC schema-init-drops-existing-rows ===\n\n=== AC schema-init-recreates-usable-schema ===\nIntent: After `ledger init` runs a second time, the recreated tables accept a new row.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\nfrom persistence import open_database\n\ndsn = os.path.join(tempfile.mkdtemp(), \"ledger.db\")\nos.environ[\"DATABASE_URL\"] = dsn\n\nfirst_code, first_out, first_err = cli_runner(\"init\")\nassert first_code == 0\nsecond_code, second_out, second_err = cli_runner(\"init\")\nassert second_code == 0\nassert second_out == \"\"\n\ndb = open_database(dsn)\ndb.accounts.create(account=\"INITB\", name=\"After Init\")\nassert len(db.accounts.list()) == 1\n=== END AC schema-init-recreates-usable-schema ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- `init` does not read any file under `sources/`.\n- `init` never writes to standard output.\n- Schema objects are created only through the generated `schema.sql`, never by inline SQL in this handler.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Schema-Count.md ===\n# FEATURE: Schema Count\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Implements `ledger count`, which prints the row count of each ledger table. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Schema-Init.md, FEATURE-CLI-Output.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides    | ledger count |\n| Consumes    | open_database, write_csv |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`ledger count` tells an operator how many rows each table holds, which is how the seed loads are checked.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Prints `table,rows`, then one line each for `accounts`, `categories`, and `transactions`, in that order.\n- Each count is the number of rows returned by that table's list operation in `DATABASE.md`. No count is stored or hardcoded.\n- The output is written through `write_csv`, so the contract in FEATURE-CLI-Output.md applies.\n- Exits 0 on success.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC == [\"accounts\", \"categories\", \"transactions\"]\n=== END AC schema-count-header-and-order ===\n\n=== AC schema-count-matches-stored-rows ===\nIntent: Each count equals the number of rows the store expected\n=== END AC schema-count-matches-stored-rows ===\n\n=== AC schema-count-zero-after-init ===\nIntent: On a freshly initialized store, every table count is zero.\n\nimport os\nimport all(int(row[1]) == 0 for row in rows)\n=== END AC schema-count-zero-after-init ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- No count value is hardcoded in this handler; every count comes from a query.\n- `count` takes no arguments and does not modify any table.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CLI-Dispatch.md ===\n# FEATURE: CLI Dispatch\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Routes `ledger` command words to their handlers and rejects usage errors with exit 2 before any database access. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CLI-Platform.md |\n| Provides    | ledger <command> routing, usage error exit 2 |\n| Consumes    | cli.main, cli_runner |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery `ledger` invocation is either handed to the handler its story owns or rejected as a usage error. Nothing else happens first.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The command word, sub-verb, and argument count are checked against the Commands table before any handler or database access runs.\n- An unknown command, unknown sub-verb, unknown table, unknown report, or wrong argument count exits 2 with exactly one line on standard error and nothing on standard output.\n- A correctly shaped command is passed to its handler, and the handler's exit code is returned unchanged.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC cli-dispatch-usage-errors-exit-two ===\nIntent: Each unknown command, sub-verb, table, report, or BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Command-Transaction.md ===\n# FEATURE: Command Transaction\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Runs every ledger command inside exactly one database transaction that commits on success and rolls back on failure. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Database-Location.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides    | run_command(db, command) |\n| Consumes    | open_database, db.accounts.create, db.accounts.list |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery command that reads or writes the store runs inside one transaction. A command either completes in full or changes nothing.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `run_command(db, command)` opens one transaction on `db`, calls `command(db)` once, commits when the call returns, and returns the value the command returned.\n- If `command` raises, every write made through `db` during that call is rolled back, and the exception propagates unchanged.\n- A persistence error raised while the transaction is open, including a write lock held by another connection, rolls back the same way and propagates. The CLI maps it to exit 1 through FEATURE-Operational-Errors.md.\n- The wrapper does not commit or roll back inside a command body. Commands never open their own transaction.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC command-transaction-commits ===\nIntent: A command that returns normally has its write persisted and its return value passed back to the caller.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom transaction import run_command\n\naccount_code = \"TXNA1\"\naccount_name = \"Commit Test\"\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    result = run_command(db, lambda d: d.accounts.create(account=account_code, name=account_name))\n    assert result.account == account_code\n    matches = [row for row in db.accounts.list() if row.account == account_code]\n    assert len(matches) == 1\n    assert matches[0].name == account_name\n=== END AC command-transaction-commits ===\n\n=== AC command-transaction-rolls-back-all-writes ===\nIntent: When a command raises after two writes, neither write persists and the exception reaches the caller.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom transaction import run_command\n\nfirst_code = \"TXNB1\"\nsecond_code = \"TXNB2\"\n\nclass Abort(Exception):\n    pass\n\ndef write_then_fail(d):\n    d.accounts.create(account=first_code, name=\"Rollback First\")\n    d.accounts.create(account=second_code, name=\"Rollback Second\")\n    raise Abort(\"forced after two writes\")\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    before = len(db.accounts.list())\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        run_command(db, write_then_fail)\n    except Abort:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n    after = db.accounts.list()\n    assert len(after) == before\n    assert all(row.account not in (first_code, second_code) for row in after)\n=== END AC command-transaction-rolls-back-all-writes ===\n\n=== AC command-transaction-propagates-exception ===\nIntent: The exception raised by a failing command is the same object the caller receives, not a wrapped or swallowed error.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom transaction import run_command\n\nraised_error = RuntimeError(\"forced by criterion\")\n\ndef fail(d):\n    raise raised_error\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    try:\n        run_command(db, fail)\n    except RuntimeError as caught:\n        assert caught is raised_error\n    else:\n        raise AssertionError(\"run_command swallowed the command's exception\")\n=== END AC command-transaction-propagates-exception ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- No module begins or commits a transaction outside `run_command`.\n- A failed command never leaves a partial write in any table.\n- No command body commits its own work.\n- Application code reaches the store only through the `persistence` package, never through a database driver.\n=== END ARTIFACT
